# Consideration of funding proposals – Addendum IX

## Funding proposal package for FP267

### Summary

The addendum contains the following seven parts:

1. A funding proposal titled "Seeking up-excitation-based approaches to managing photosynthesis in C4 crops" (FP267).
2. A letter signed by the scientific departmental advisory board head.
3. Institutional and social reports/disclosures.
4. Independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment, including the Independent Technical Advisory Panel's recommendations; and
5. Disclosure.

This document is prepared to provide additional information on the funding proposal FP267. It includes assessments and recommendations from various bodies involved in the evaluation process.

Executing Entity: South African National Biodiversity Institute (SANBI)

Implementing Agency: South African National Parks (SANParks) and the Eastern Cape Department of Economic Development, Environmental Affairs and Tourism (DEDEAT).

### A.21 Project summary (max 700 words approximately 13 pages):

The project will focus on drought resilience through extreme-weather events and biodiversity across South Africa. It will increase resilience to climate change by improving water security and enhancing ecosystem services. The project will also improve the livelihoods of local communities, particularly women and youth.

#### 1. Objective
To enhance the resilience of key ecosystems and species to climate change impacts in South Africa through improved water security and biodiversity conservation.

#### 2. Description
The project will focus on drought resilience through extreme-weather events and biodiversity across South Africa. It will increase resilience to climate change by improving water security and enhancing ecosystem services. The project will also improve the livelihoods of local communities, particularly women and youth.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.2: PAGE 1

## A

### Resources with disaster risk reduction (DRR) interventions in a fairly narrow sense and emerging approaches to address climate change adaptation and mitigation.

- **Disaster risk reduction** is an important component of climate change adaptation.
- **Climate change adaptation** seeks to help people cope with the adverse effects of climate change, including sea-level rise and more extreme weather events. It is targeted towards at-risk areas such as Small Islands.
- These approaches require modules that incorporate DRR into their design.

### This proposal recommends that EIA and co-financing be used to fund projects that are not normally financed by the GCF. These projects should be designed to address climate change adaptation, disaster risk reduction, and sustainable development.

#### The following table provides an overview of the types of projects that can be funded:

| Type of Project | Description |
|-----------------|--------------|
| **Disaster Risk Reduction** | Projects aimed at reducing the impact of natural disasters. |
| **Climate Change Adaptation** | Projects designed to help communities adapt to the effects of climate change. |
| **Sustainable Development** | Projects that promote sustainable development and environmental protection. |

### The primary objective is to support projects that address disaster risk reduction, climate change adaptation, and sustainable development.

#### Key objectives include:

- **Reducing vulnerability**: Reduce the impact of disasters on communities.
- **Improving resilience**: Enhance the ability of communities to cope with climate change.
- **Promoting sustainability**: Support projects that promote sustainable development.
- **Enhancing capacity**: Build the capacity of communities to adapt to climate change.

### The proposal addresses GCF's fiduciary risk management requirements and its ecosystem impact for projects that are not normally financed by the GCF. It also supports the development of new technologies and approaches to disaster risk reduction, climate change adaptation, and sustainable development.

- **Reducing vulnerability**: Reduce the impact of disasters on communities.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL v.1.2: PAGE 8

- **Section A**: Limited transmission of El Niño and La Niña (EN) events to South Africa. Many of the EN and ENSO (El Niño Southern Oscillation) events have been well documented and the impact on climate change is well understood. The GCF project aligns with South Africa's Climate Change Act (Act 12 of 2021), which aligns a green, low-carbon, climate-resilient development path. This includes the National Climate Change Adaptation Strategy (NCCAS) (DFFE, 2017), the Climate Change Response Plan (DFFE, 2019), and South Africa's Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs). The project aligns with the Paris Agreement and the United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs).

## Project Programme Information

### B. Climate Change Impacts, 1000 words, approximately 2 pages

#### National Climate Context

South Africa is a highly vulnerable country to climate change impacts. Temperature increases ranging from 1.5-3°C in summer and -2-4°C in winter (IPCC, 2018) are projected by the end of this century. Rainfall over South Africa at high elevations, such as in the Drakensberg Mountains, is projected to increase by up to 20% by 2050. However, rainfall in low-lying areas is expected to decrease by up to 30%. The frequency and intensity of extreme weather events are also expected to increase (IPCC, 2018). These changes will have significant impacts on water resources, agriculture, health, and biodiversity.

#### Project Objectives

The project aims to identify climate change trends in the targeted districts municipalities (DMs).

| District | Climate Change Trends | Objective |
|----------|-----------------------|------------|
| **Eastern Cape** | Increasing temperatures, decreased rainfall | Identify impacts on agriculture and water resources |
| **Free State** | Increased frequency of extreme weather events | Assess impacts on infrastructure and health |
| **Gauteng** | Urban heat island effects | Evaluate urban adaptation strategies |

### C. Project Activities

The project will include the following activities:

1. **Data Collection**: Gather historical climate data from various sources.
2. **Analysis**: Conduct statistical analysis to identify trends.
3. **Stakeholder Engagement**: Engage with local communities and stakeholders to understand impacts.
4. **Reporting**: Prepare reports on findings and recommendations.

### D. Project Timeline

The project will run for 2 years, starting in January 2023 and ending in December 2025.

| Activity | Start Date | End Date |
|----------|-------------|----------|
| Data Collection | Jan 2023 | Jun 2023 |
| Analysis | Jul 2023 | Dec 2023 |
| Stakeholder Engagement | Jan 2024 | Jun 2024 |
| Reporting | Jul 2024 | Dec 2025 |

### E. Budget

The project budget is R10 million, with the following allocations:

- **Data Collection**: R2 million
- **Analysis**: R3 million
- **Stakeholder Engagement**: R3 million
- **Reporting**: R2 million

## Impact of climate change and increased climate variability on communities

The greatest impacts are projected to be in areas with high population density and low adaptive capacity. These areas include urban centers, coastal regions, and small island developing states (SIDS). The impact will vary depending on the services that need reducing the levels of impact of these hazards.

## Table 5: Summary of benefits, impacts and adaptation activities for the project

### Adaptation Activity

| Category | Benefit/Impact | Adaptation Activities |
|----------|-----------------|-----------------------|
| **Health** | - Reduced cases of malaria due to improved drainage systems. <br> - Improved health services in rural areas. | - Construction of new drainage systems. <br> - Upgrading of existing health facilities. |
| **Agriculture** | - Increased crop yields due to improved irrigation systems. <br> - Diversification of crops leading to increased food security. | - Introduction of new irrigation systems. <br> - Training farmers on climate-smart agriculture practices. |
| **Water Resources** | - Improved water availability for domestic and agricultural use. <br> - Reduced waterborne diseases due to improved sanitation. | - Construction of new dams and boreholes. <br> - Implementation of rainwater harvesting systems. |
| **Ecosystem Services** | - Increased biodiversity due to restored wetlands. <br> - Improved ecosystem services such as carbon sequestration. | - Restoration of degraded wetlands. <br> - Planting of indigenous tree species. |

### Theory of Change: Impacts and Adaptation Outcomes (10-year view)

#### Problem Statement

- The frequency and intensity of droughts, extreme seasonal events and additional stresses South Africa face increased risks to livelihoods and economic development.
- This will affect household food and water security as well as increasing the risk of flooding for South Africans living in low-lying areas. It also increases the risk of waterborne diseases and other health issues.

#### Long-term Outcomes

- Improved resilience to climate change impacts through enhanced adaptive capacity.
- Increased agricultural productivity and improved livelihoods.
- Enhanced ecosystem services and biodiversity conservation.
- Strengthened early warning systems and disaster risk management.

16. Climate change threatens several ongoing anthropogenic activities including overgrazing, deforestation, mining, poaching, and illegal fishing. These activities have led to the degradation of natural resources and the loss of biodiversity. Due to women's strong reliance on natural resources, they are disproportionately affected by climate change impacts. Women in rural areas face increased vulnerability due to their dependence on natural resources for livelihoods.

17. Similarly, youth are just as vulnerable to degradation and climate change as seniors and future generations depend on the environment for their survival. Youth are particularly vulnerable to climate change because of their limited access to information and education about climate change impacts. They also face challenges in accessing opportunities that can help them adapt to a changing world.

18. The impact of climate change is exacerbated by the lack of preparedness and capacity to respond to extreme weather events such as droughts, floods, and storms. This is especially true for communities living in remote areas with limited access to resources and infrastructure. These communities are often the most vulnerable to the impacts of climate change.

19. The impact of climate change on agriculture is significant. Climate change affects crop yields, reduces water availability, and increases the frequency and intensity of extreme weather events such as droughts, floods, and storms. This has a direct impact on food security and nutrition, which in turn affects the health and well-being of communities.

20. The impact of climate change on agriculture is significant. Climate change affects crop yields, reduces water availability, and increases the frequency and intensity of extreme weather events such as droughts, floods, and storms. This has a direct impact on food security and nutrition, which in turn affects the health and well-being of communities.

## C

The proposal aims to enhance the capacity of developing countries to integrate adaptation and mitigation measures into their national development planning processes.

### 25.
The proposed Eco-DRR project aligns with the vulnerability stance, on environmental and natural resource management, and is consistent with the objectives of the GCF. The project will be transformative in the way it:
- Enhances the resilience of vulnerable communities to climate change impacts by integrating total and integrated knowledge and science, and also integrates climate change adaptation and DRR.
- Enhances the sub-national DRR function that is able to factor ecological/biophysical considerations into planning and implementation.
- Enhances the integration of national Eco-DRR through improved efficiency in public sector land and water resources management.
- Enhances the integration of local knowledge, practices, and traditional ecological knowledge (TEK) through participatory approaches.
- Enhances the integration of gender equality and social inclusion by creating adaptive management, cross-sectoral linkages, and self-organized learning systems.

### 26.
The project aims to directly support that integrated governance structures, especially women as a leadership to adapt to climate change. This will be achieved through the following:
- The project will enhance gender equity outcomes throughout the project cycle, support gender equality and social inclusion (GESI) objectives, and ensure that GESI is mainstreamed in all aspects of the project.
- The project will directly support the integration of gender equality and social inclusion by creating adaptive management, cross-sectoral linkages, and self-organized learning systems.

### 27.
The project will be implemented through a participatory approach that continues the achievement of the primary adaptation outcome:
- Enhance the capacity of developing countries to integrate adaptation and mitigation measures into their national development planning processes.

### 8.2 Outcomes Mapping to GCF Results Areas and Co-benefit Integration

#### Figure 3: Theory of Change for the proposed project

**GCF Results Area (R.A.) 1 - 6**

- **R.A. 1**: Disaster Risk Reduction
    - **Sub-Outcome 1**: Enhanced community resilience to climate change impacts.
        - **Discrete Outcome 1**: Increased awareness and knowledge of climate risks among communities.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of community members trained in disaster risk reduction.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in community participation in DRR activities.

    - **Sub-Outcome 2**: Improved infrastructure for disaster preparedness.
        - **Discrete Outcome 2**: Strengthened early warning systems and emergency response mechanisms.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of functional early warning systems installed.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage reduction in response time to disasters.

    - **Sub-Outcome 3**: Enhanced community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM).
        - **Discrete Outcome 3**: Establishment and operationalization of CBDRM committees.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of active CBDRM committees.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in community involvement in DRR planning.

- **R.A. 2**: Climate Change Mitigation
    - **Sub-Outcome 1**: Reduced greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions from key sectors.
        - **Discrete Outcome 1**: Implementation of energy-efficient technologies.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of energy-efficient devices installed.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage reduction in GHG emissions.

    - **Sub-Outcome 2**: Promotion of renewable energy sources.
        - **Discrete Outcome 2**: Increased adoption of renewable energy solutions.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of households using renewable energy sources.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in renewable energy usage.

    - **Sub-Outcome 3**: Enhanced forest conservation and reforestation efforts.
        - **Discrete Outcome 3**: Establishment of new forests and protection of existing ones.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of hectares reforested.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in forest cover.

**GCF Results Area (R.A.) 7 - 9**

- **R.A. 7**: Climate Change Adaptation
    - **Sub-Outcome 1**: Improved agricultural practices and technologies.
        - **Discrete Outcome 1**: Adoption of climate-smart agriculture techniques.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of farmers trained in climate-smart agriculture.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in crop yields.

    - **Sub-Outcome 2**: Enhanced water management systems.
        - **Discrete Outcome 2**: Implementation of efficient irrigation methods and rainwater harvesting.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of functional water management systems installed.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage reduction in water scarcity issues.

    - **Sub-Outcome 3**: Strengthened community-based adaptation strategies.
        - **Discrete Outcome 3**: Establishment and operationalization of community adaptation plans.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of active community adaptation plans.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in community resilience to climate change.

- **R.A. 8**: Gender Equality
    - **Sub-Outcome 1**: Empowerment of women and girls in climate action.
        - **Discrete Outcome 1**: Increased participation of women in decision-making processes related to climate change.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of women participating in climate-related meetings.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in female leadership roles.

    - **Sub-Outcome 2**: Enhanced access to resources and opportunities for women.
        - **Discrete Outcome 2**: Improved access to finance, education, and health services for women.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of women beneficiaries of climate-related projects.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in women's economic empowerment.

    - **Sub-Outcome 3**: Promotion of gender-responsive climate policies.
        - **Discrete Outcome 3**: Development and implementation of gender-responsive climate policies.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of gender-responsive policies adopted.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage improvement in gender equality indicators.

- **R.A. 9**: Co-benefits
    - **Sub-Outcome 1**: Improved public health outcomes.
        - **Discrete Outcome 1**: Reduction in climate-related health risks.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of climate-related health incidents reported.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage reduction in climate-related health issues.

    - **Sub-Outcome 2**: Enhanced environmental sustainability.
        - **Discrete Outcome 2**: Implementation of sustainable practices and technologies.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of sustainable practices adopted.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage improvement in environmental indicators.

    - **Sub-Outcome 3**: Strengthened community livelihoods.
        - **Discrete Outcome 3**: Establishment of sustainable livelihood programs.
            - **Indicator 1**: Number of households benefiting from livelihood programs.
            - **Indicator 2**: Percentage increase in household income.

append to environmental rehabilitation and management of the country. The Eco-UNFCCC interventions will build upon existing efforts and support the implementation of the National Adaptation Plan (NAP) process and other activities. These interventions will be developed in close collaboration with line ministries, local governments, civil society organizations, and other stakeholders. They will address the challenges of climate change impacts on ecosystems, biodiversity, and natural resources. The interventions will also enhance the resilience of communities to climate change through improved management of natural resources, and ensure that vulnerable ecosystems are protected and restored.

### Ability 1.1.1 Establish local real project delivery funds, government arrangements and capacity for implementation

- Funding is being sought from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and European Union (EU). The proposal will facilitate local level interventions to enhance climate resilience and adaptation.

## Table 1: Summary of proposed ex-ante GCF-funded interventions to support the protection of critical assets in the region

| Type of Intervention | Risks Mitigated | Beneficiaries | Year |
|----------------------|-----------------|---------------|------|
| Clearing of debris and waste from beaches | Coastal communities | 10,000 people | 2023 |
| Development of a community-based disaster management system | Coastal communities | 5,000 households | 2024 |
| Establishment of an early warning system for coastal communities | Coastal communities | 10,000 people | 2025 |

---

### Objective 1.2: Develop policies and sustainable economic tools to facilitate rapid recovery, build resilience, and support the protection of critical assets in the region

The objective is to develop policies and sustainable economic tools that will support the protection of critical assets in the region. This includes the development of a comprehensive policy framework for disaster risk reduction (DRR) and climate change adaptation (CCA). The policy framework should include measures such as:

- **Disaster Risk Reduction**: Policies aimed at reducing disaster risks, including early warning systems, community-based disaster management, and infrastructure improvements.
- **Climate Change Adaptation**: Policies to support the implementation of CCA measures, such as climate-smart agriculture, sustainable water management, and renewable energy adoption.

The policy framework should be developed in consultation with relevant stakeholders, including government agencies (e.g., Department of Environment, Climate Change Agency), non-governmental organizations (NGOs), and community groups. The policies should also align with international best practices and standards for DRR and CCA.

---

### Objective 1.3: Enhance the capacity of local governments to implement climate change adaptation measures

The objective is to enhance the capacity of local governments to implement climate change adaptation measures. This includes providing training, technical assistance, and financial support to local governments to develop and implement CCA plans. The activities may include:

- **Training and Capacity Building**: Providing training on CCA planning, implementation, and monitoring for local government officials.
- **Technical Assistance**: Offering expert advice and support to local governments in the development of CCA plans.
- **Financial Support**: Providing grants or low-interest loans to support the implementation of CCA measures.

The capacity-building activities should be tailored to the specific needs and priorities of each local government. The activities should also promote regional cooperation and knowledge sharing among local governments.

---

### Objective 2: Develop a comprehensive strategy for the integration of climate change adaptation into national development planning

The objective is to develop a comprehensive strategy for integrating climate change adaptation (CCA) into national development planning. This includes the development of policies, plans, and programs that incorporate CCA measures across all sectors of the economy. The activities may include:

- **Policy Development**: Developing national policies that promote CCA integration into sectoral plans and budgets.
- **Plan Development**: Creating long-term development plans that include CCA measures and targets.
- **Program Development**: Designing programs that support the implementation of CCA measures at the national level.

The strategy should be developed in consultation with relevant stakeholders, including government agencies (e.g., Department of Environment, Climate Change Agency), NGOs, and community groups. The strategy should also align with international best practices and standards for CCA integration into development planning.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.3.1 - PAGE 17

## Objective 1: Local governments and communities implement approved Eco-DRR proposals and response measures

### 58. Local governments and local authorities will be encouraged to develop and implement their own Eco-DRR projects, including those supported by the GCF and other funding sources.

### 59. The GCF will support the development of local Eco-DRR projects through technical assistance, capacity building, and financial support.

### 60. Local governments will be required to submit an annual report on the progress of their Eco-DRR projects to the GCF.

## Objective 2: Strengthen the capacity of local governments and communities to implement approved Eco-DRR proposals

### 61. The capacity of local governments and communities to implement Eco-DRR proposals will be strengthened through training, workshops, and other capacity-building activities.

### 62. Local governments and communities will be provided with access to technical expertise and resources to support the implementation of their Eco-DRR projects.

## Objective 3: Enhance the resilience of local governments and communities to climate change

### 63. The GCF will support the development and implementation of climate-resilient infrastructure in local governments and communities, including early warning systems, flood protection measures, and other climate adaptation measures.

### 64. Local governments and communities will be encouraged to integrate climate risk management into their planning and decision-making processes.

## Objective 4: Promote gender equality and social inclusion in Eco-DRR projects

### 65. The GCF will support the development and implementation of Eco-DRR projects that promote gender equality and social inclusion, including those that target vulnerable groups such as women, children, and persons with disabilities.

### 66. Local governments and communities will be encouraged to adopt gender-responsive and socially inclusive approaches in their Eco-DRR projects.

## Objective 5: Enhance the monitoring and evaluation of Eco-DRR projects

### 67. The GCF will support the development and implementation of a robust monitoring and evaluation system for Eco-DRR projects, including indicators to track progress and measure impact.

### 68. Local governments and communities will be required to submit regular reports on the progress of their Eco-DRR projects, including data on key performance indicators.

## Objective 6: Strengthen partnerships and collaboration

### 69. The GCF will support the development of partnerships and collaborations between local governments, communities, and other stakeholders, including national and international organizations, to enhance the implementation of Eco-DRR projects.

### 70. Local governments and communities will be encouraged to engage with other stakeholders in their region and globally to share best practices and lessons learned.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.3.1

## Appendix A.1.1 Developing a partnership arrangement and memorandum of understanding for Eco-DRR data portal and data center

Developing a partnership arrangement and memorandum of understanding (MOU) for the Eco-DRR data portal and data center is essential to perpetuate a large financial mechanism that can promote multi-stakeholder engagement in the Eco-DRR. Partnerships will be vital to ensure the sustainability, relevance, and effectiveness of the Eco-DRR data portal and data center. The MOU should include:

- A clear description of roles and responsibilities
- A commitment to the principles of transparency, accountability, and mutual respect
- A mechanism for resolving disputes

The partnership arrangement should also include a provision for the establishment of a governance structure that will oversee the implementation of the Eco-DRR data portal and data center. This structure could include representatives from:

- The Global Environment Facility (GEF)
- The World Bank (WB)
- The United Nations Development Programme (UNDP)
- The United Nations Office for Disaster Risk Reduction (UNISDR)
- The International Federation of Red Cross and Red Crescent Societies (IFRC)

The MOU should also include a provision for the establishment of a governance structure that will oversee the implementation of the Eco-DRR data portal and data center. This structure could include representatives from:

- The Global Environment Facility (GEF)
- The World Bank (WB)
- The United Nations Development Programme (UNDP)
- The United Nations Office for Disaster Risk Reduction (UNISDR)
- The International Federation of Red Cross and Red Crescent Societies (IFRC)

- A commitment to the principles of transparency, accountability, and mutual respect
- A mechanism for resolving disputes

---

**Figure 8: Project Governance Model**

1. **SANABI** acts as the executing entity responsible for managing and implementing the Green Climate Fund (GCF) projects. SANABI is a South African National Biodiversity Institute that has been accredited by the GCF to implement projects.
2. **SANABI** through its Project Management Office (PMO), will manage the project implementation, including the development of project documents, procurement processes, and financial management.
3. The PMO will be supported by a Project Management Unit (PMU) that will provide technical assistance and oversight to ensure that the project is implemented in accordance with GCF policies and procedures.
4. **SANABI** as an executing entity, will work closely with the National Designated Authority (NDA), which is responsible for managing the relationship between the country and the GCF.
5. The PMU will be supported by a Project Management Office (PMO), which manages technical experts to ensure that the project is implemented in accordance with the approved project documents.
6. **SANABI** will work closely with the National Designated Authority (NDA) to ensure alignment of the project with national priorities and policies.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

## Figure 3: Expenditure Funding Flows

The GCF was initially funded by voluntary contributions from donor countries and other sources. The funding flows are illustrated in the following figure:

![Figure 3](image_url)

### Key Points:
1. **Initial Funding**: The initial funding for the GCF comes from voluntary contributions.
2. **Accreditation**: Entities seeking accreditation must demonstrate their capacity to manage funds effectively.
3. **Funding Agreements**: Accredited entities enter into funding agreements with the GCF, specifying the use of funds and reporting requirements.
4. **Project Implementation**: Funds are used for implementing projects that support climate change mitigation and adaptation efforts.

### Expenditure Funding Flows:
- **Accreditation Fee**: A fee is charged to accredited entities.
- **Administrative Costs**: Administrative costs are covered by the GCF.
- **Project Funding**: The majority of funds go towards project implementation.

## GCF's Role in Financial Management

The GCF plays a crucial role in managing and tracking financial flows, ensuring transparency and accountability. Key aspects include:

1. **Accreditation Process**: Ensures entities have the capacity to manage funds effectively.
2. **Funding Agreements**: Clearly outline the use of funds and reporting requirements.
3. **Project Monitoring**: Regular monitoring to ensure funds are used as intended.

### Financial Management Challenges:
- **Volatility in Funding**: Voluntary contributions can be volatile, impacting financial planning.
- **Complex Reporting Requirements**: Ensuring accurate and timely reporting from accredited entities.
- **Risk Management**: Managing risks associated with project implementation.

## GCF's Impact on Climate Finance

The GCF has significantly contributed to climate finance globally. Key achievements include:

1. **Funding Scale**: The GCF has mobilized over $10 billion in funding, supporting more than 2,500 projects.
2. **Increased Ambition**: The demand for financing from developing countries has increased, reflecting a growing recognition of the need to address climate change.
3. **Private Sector Engagement**: The GCF has facilitated greater engagement with the private sector, leveraging additional funding and expertise.

### Future Outlook:
- **Increasing Demand**: The demand for climate finance is expected to increase, driven by the need to address climate change more effectively.
- **Policy Alignment**: Greater alignment of climate finance with national policies will be crucial for effective implementation.
- **Sustainability**: Ensuring sustainability in funding sources and project outcomes will be essential for long-term success.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1 | PAGE 3

The Eco-DRIF is a unique opportunity to leverage funding from government, cross-sector project DGR grants, which are currently available in South Africa. The COVID-19 pandemic has had a significant impact on the livelihoods of many people and communities across the country. Also, we have learned that the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic is still present. South Africa is committed to taking action against climate change and implementing projects such as the Eco-DRIF project. The Government GCF is poised to be valued at being able to provide funding for projects that will support Ecosystem-based Adaptation (EbA) and Eco-DRIF interventions. The Government of South Africa has also committed to providing funding for these projects over a period of 5 years. As such, this is significantly reduced the funding available to support EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions.

## Executive Summary

The Government GCF funding for the Eco-DRIF project is intended to be implemented by the Department of Environment, Forestry and Fisheries (DEFF) over a period of 5 years. The project will be implemented in two phases: Phase I will focus on the development of the project and Phase II will focus on the implementation of the project.

### Objectives

The objectives of the Eco-DRIF project are to:

- Support the development and implementation of EbA interventions.
- Support the development and implementation of Eco-DRIF interventions.
- Support the development and implementation of climate change adaptation projects.
- Support the development and implementation of climate change mitigation projects.
- Support the development and implementation of climate change resilience projects.

### Implementation

The project will be implemented in two phases:

#### Phase I: Development Phase

The objectives of this phase are to:

- Conduct a baseline assessment of the current state of EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions in South Africa.
- Develop a comprehensive plan for the development and implementation of EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions.
- Develop a detailed budget for the project.

#### Phase II: Implementation Phase

The objectives of this phase are to:

- Implement the EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions as outlined in the comprehensive plan developed during Phase I.
- Monitor and evaluate the progress of the EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions.
- Conduct a final evaluation of the project.

### Funding

The Government GCF funding for the Eco-DRIF project is intended to be used for:

- The development and implementation of EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions.
- The monitoring and evaluation of EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions.
- The conduct of baseline assessments, comprehensive planning, budgeting, and other related activities.

### Timeline

The project is expected to be implemented over a period of 5 years. The timeline for the implementation of the project is as follows:

- Phase I: Development Phase (Year 1)
- Phase II: Implementation Phase (Years 2-5)

### Stakeholders

The stakeholders in the Eco-DRIF project include:

- The Department of Environment, Forestry and Fisheries (DEFF)
- The Government GCF
- The beneficiaries of EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions
- Other relevant government departments and agencies
- Non-governmental organizations (NGOs) and civil society organizations (CSOs)
- The private sector

### Conclusion

The Eco-DRIF project is a unique opportunity to leverage funding from the Government GCF to support EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions in South Africa. The project will be implemented over a period of 5 years, with Phase I focusing on the development of the project and Phase II focusing on the implementation of the project. The objectives of the project are to support the development and implementation of EbA and Eco-DRIF interventions, as well as climate change adaptation, mitigation, and resilience projects.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1: PAGE 8

## C

While in keeping of the management and maintenance of ecological infrastructure. This would be better viewed as the establishment of a fund to support the management and maintenance of ecological infrastructure. The establishment of such a fund, which is supported by an ecological infrastructure tax has been following for some time. The tax base could be the value added tax (VAT) on construction materials and equipment used in ecological infrastructure projects. This would be a small percentage of the cost of building new homes or renovating old ones. It could also be a percentage of the cost of building new roads, bridges, and other public works. The tax rate would be set at 1% for residential construction and 2% for commercial construction.

### 305

The establishment of such a fund has been supported by the European Union (EU) and the United Nations (UN). The EU has provided funding for the establishment of such a fund in several countries, including the United Kingdom. The UN has also provided funding for the establishment of such a fund in several countries, including the United States.

### 306

The establishment of such a fund would be a significant step forward in the management and maintenance of ecological infrastructure. It would provide a stable source of funding for the management and maintenance of ecological infrastructure. It would also provide a mechanism for the collection of data on the use of ecological infrastructure. This data could be used to improve the management and maintenance of ecological infrastructure.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## C

Participating with **COOFA**, **NDMC** and **SALUG** is essential in ensuring that these agreements successfully reach their intended outcomes. The project will be implemented using integrated approaches to achieve socio-economic development and environmental sustainability. We will promote the use of improved technologies, practices, and management systems to enhance productivity, profitability, and sustainability. **NDMC** is responsible for ensuring the **National Framework** for Climate Services (NFCS) is effectively implemented in the project area. This includes climate change-related risk assessments, early warning systems, and climate-smart agriculture strategies. It is also for the development of climate-resilient infrastructure and the integration of climate change adaptation into government activities, particularly in the implementation of national, regional, and local climate action plans.

### Project benefits on climate change

1. **Climate Change Mitigation**: The project will contribute to reducing greenhouse gas emissions by promoting sustainable practices such as agroforestry, conservation agriculture, and efficient use of energy.
2. **Climate Change Adaptation**: The project will enhance the resilience of communities to climate change through improved water management, early warning systems, and climate-smart agriculture.

### Project benefits on gender equality

1. **Gender Equality**: Women will be actively involved in all stages of the project implementation process. This includes participation in decision-making processes, access to resources, and capacity-building activities.
2. **Economic Empowerment**: The project will support women's economic empowerment through the provision of microfinance services, business development training, and market access.

### Organizational and institutional capacity

1. **Capacity Building**: The project will build the capacity of local communities and institutions to implement and sustain the project activities.
2. **Institutional Strengthening**: The project will support the strengthening of local government institutions to effectively manage and monitor the project activities.

### Project implementation arrangements

1. **Project Management Unit (PMU)**: A specialized unit will be established to oversee the implementation of projects undertaken during the duration of the Eco-DEF project.
2. **Partnerships**: The project will establish partnerships with relevant stakeholders, including government agencies, non-governmental organizations, and private sector entities.

### Financial Information

|---------------------|--------------|----------|

**Note**: COOFA is a partner entity that is required to promote cooperative governance activities across and departments. MOUs will be used to ensure the effective implementation of the project.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a financial mechanism under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). It was established to support projects and programs in developing countries aimed at mitigating greenhouse gas emissions and adapting to climate change. The GCF operates through accredited entities, which include multilateral development banks, UN agencies, and other specialized institutions.

## Project Description

The project aims to enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities to climate change by implementing adaptation measures. It focuses on improving water management systems, enhancing agricultural practices, and promoting renewable energy solutions. The project will be implemented in collaboration with local governments and community organizations to ensure sustainability and long-term impact.

### Objectives

1. **Objective 1**: To improve access to clean water for at least 50,000 people by implementing rainwater harvesting systems.
2. **Objective 2**: To increase agricultural productivity by 30% through the adoption of climate-smart farming techniques.
3. **Objective 3**: To provide renewable energy solutions to at least 100 households, reducing their dependence on fossil fuels.

### Implementation Strategy

The project will be implemented in three phases:

1. **Phase 1: Planning and Preparation**
   - Conduct a comprehensive needs assessment.
   - Develop detailed project plans and budgets.
   - Engage with local stakeholders for buy-in and support.

2. **Phase 2: Implementation**
   - Construct rainwater harvesting systems.
   - Train farmers on climate-smart agriculture practices.
   - Install renewable energy solutions.

3. **Phase 3: Monitoring and Evaluation**
   - Monitor progress against set targets.
   - Evaluate the impact of implemented measures.
   - Adjust strategies as needed based on feedback and results.

### Budget

The total budget for the project is $10 million, with the following allocations:

- Water management systems: $2.5 million
- Agricultural practices: $4 million
- Renewable energy solutions: $3.5 million
- Monitoring and evaluation: $0.5 million

## Conclusion

The GCF funding proposal outlines a comprehensive approach to addressing climate change impacts in vulnerable communities. By focusing on water management, agricultural practices, and renewable energy, the project aims to enhance resilience and improve livelihoods.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## PAGE 14

The GCF project aligns with South Africa’s Climate Change Act (Act 12 of 2021) which places a clear responsibility on government to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and build resilience to climate change. The National Climate Change Response Department (NCCRD), under the Department of Environment, Forestry and Fisheries (DEFF), is responsible for the climate change agenda in South Africa.

### South Africa’s response to the urgency to not only adapt but also mitigate climate change

South Africa has committed to reducing its greenhouse gas emissions by 34% below business-as-usual levels by 2025, 42% by 2030 and 61% by 2040. These targets are in line with the Paris Agreement’s goal of limiting global warming to well below 2°C above pre-industrial levels and pursuing efforts to limit the increase to 1.5°C.

### South Africa’s Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs)

South Africa’s NDCs were submitted in November 2020, and updated in August 2021. These NDCs include a range of mitigation actions that are expected to reduce emissions by 398 MtCO2e by 2030 compared to business-as-usual. The NDCs also include adaptation measures that will help build resilience to climate change.

### South Africa’s Climate Change Response Policy

South Africa’s Climate Change Response Policy was approved in November 2017 and provides a framework for the implementation of the Paris Agreement. The policy sets out a long-term vision for reducing greenhouse gas emissions and building resilience to climate change, with a focus on sustainable development and economic growth.

### South Africa’s National Adaptation Plan (NAP)

South Africa’s NAP was developed in 2015 and provides a framework for the implementation of adaptation measures. The NAP sets out a long-term vision for reducing greenhouse gas emissions and building resilience to climate change, with a focus on sustainable development and economic growth.

### South Africa’s National Climate Change Response Strategy

South Africa’s National Climate Change Response Strategy was developed in 2015 and provides a framework for the implementation of adaptation measures. The strategy sets out a long-term vision for reducing greenhouse gas emissions and building resilience to climate change, with a focus on sustainable development and economic growth.

The proposal aims to enhance efficiency and effectiveness of environmental public works programmes in the country by making them more effective in delivering climate resilience. - **EP Programmes:** Including Projects for Water, Working for Ecosystems, and Working for Women.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

## Table E: Potential cost per beneficiary

### Cost Benefit Analysis Across the First Included District and National

Total cost per beneficiary:

- The proposed Eco GCF project is an efficient use of GCF funding, with a total cost to benefit ratio of 1:3. This means that for every dollar spent on the project, three dollars in benefits are generated.
- The project's cost-benefit analysis was conducted using the Social Return on Investment (SROI) methodology, which is widely recognized as a robust and comprehensive approach to measuring social value.
- The total cost of the project is $10 million, with an estimated benefit of $30 million. This results in a cost-benefit ratio of 1:3.
- The project's benefits include improved livelihoods for farmers, increased agricultural productivity, and enhanced food security. These benefits are expected to last for at least 25 years.

### Table F: Cost-Benefit Analysis Results

| Year | GCF Contribution | SROI Benefit | Net Present Value |
|------|------------------|-------------|-------------------|
| 0    | $10,000,000      | $30,000,000 | $20,000,000       |
| 5    | $8,000,000       | $24,000,000 | $16,000,000       |
| 10   | $6,000,000       | $18,000,000 | $12,000,000       |

At the 5% discount rate, the proposed interventions and sites selected during feasibility determine a Net Present Value (NPV) of $14.6 million and a benefit-cost ratio (BCR) of 3.4. Demonstrating that every dollar invested results in benefits worth $3.4.

### Impact Assessment

- The project will be implemented over five years, with an additional two-year monitoring period.
- The project aims to improve the livelihoods of 10,000 households and increase agricultural productivity by 25%.
- The project will also contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), particularly SDG 1 (No Poverty) and SDG 2 (Zero Hunger).
- The project will be implemented in collaboration with local governments and communities, ensuring sustainability and long-term impact.

### Importance of GCF Funding

GCF funding is critical to implementing this project. It will enable the country's most vulnerable and remote rural households to improve their livelihoods and increase agricultural productivity.

## Objective

The budget for the current financial year (2025/26) was for net financial per (2024/25) has been reduced by 10%. This has been reflected in the proposed budget for the next financial year. The Department of Environmental Affairs is committed to ensuring that the country benefits from the Green Climate Fund (GCF). There are concerns that these budgets will be further reduced and this may impact on the ability of the department to deliver on its mandate. The department is committed to implementing development priorities, limited extensions of the water security plan, increased climate change adaptation initiatives, and the need for vehicle emissions including taking to carbon neutral charge. This has resulted in limited or no funding available to implement these projects. The current budget will impact on the ability to sustain and grow the Green Climate Fund (GCF) portfolio.

### Key outcomes of this proposal is to determine:

- **Budgeting and financial management** are models for the primary accounts of the department.
- **Funding** is required to ensure that the department can deliver on its mandate. The current budget will impact on the ability to sustain and grow the Green Climate Fund (GCF) portfolio.

### Recommendations

There is an opportunity for developing new projects that would fund these initiatives. As development funding is limited, there are opportunities to explore alternative sources of funding. The department has identified a number of projects that could be funded from the current budget. These include:

- **Funding** for the implementation of the Green Climate Fund (GCF).
- **Funding** for the implementation of the Green Climate Fund (GCF).

When the GCF funding is available, the current situation in terms of sustainable D&A is key to ensuring that the department can deliver on its mandate.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.0 PAGE 45

## E

### Objective Documentation and Strategic Direction

**Objective:** To provide technical support to countries in the development of their Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) and performance frameworks, as well as to enhance the capacity of governments to implement climate change actions and benefits of government to achieve their NDCs.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance to countries.
- Enhance capacity building for NDC implementation.
- Support the development of national climate change strategies.
- Facilitate access to finance and technology transfer.
- Promote regional cooperation on climate change.

### Green Initiative 1: Climate Finance Access

**Objective:** To enhance the ability of developing countries to access international climate finance, including the Green Climate Fund (GCF).

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for NDC preparation and implementation.
- Facilitate access to GCF resources through direct accreditation or through an accredited entity.
- Support the development of national climate change strategies.

### Green Initiative 2: Low Emission Development Strategies

**Objective:** To support countries in developing low emission development strategies (LEDS) that integrate climate change mitigation and adaptation actions into broader economic, social, and environmental policies and plans.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for LEDS development.
- Support the integration of climate change considerations into national development planning processes.
- Facilitate access to finance for LEDS implementation.

### Green Initiative 3: Climate Resilience

**Objective:** To enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities and ecosystems to the impacts of climate change, including through improved early warning systems and disaster risk reduction.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for climate resilience planning.
- Support the development and implementation of national adaptation plans (NAPs).
- Facilitate access to finance for climate resilience projects.

### Green Initiative 4: Climate Information and Early Warning Systems

**Objective:** To enhance the availability and use of climate information and early warning systems to support climate-resilient development.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for the development and implementation of climate information and early warning systems.
- Support the integration of climate information into national and local decision-making processes.
- Facilitate access to finance for climate information and early warning systems.

### Green Initiative 5: Capacity Building

**Objective:** To enhance the capacity of developing countries to effectively implement their NDCs, including through enhanced institutional arrangements and human resources development.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for capacity building.
- Support the development of national climate change institutions and policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for capacity building projects.

### Green Initiative 6: Private Sector Engagement

**Objective:** To engage the private sector in climate action, including through the development of public-private partnerships (PPPs) and other forms of collaboration.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for private sector engagement.
- Support the development of PPPs and other forms of collaboration between governments and the private sector.
- Facilitate access to finance for private sector climate action projects.

### Green Initiative 7: Technology Transfer

**Objective:** To enhance the transfer of climate technology to developing countries, including through South-South cooperation and North-South partnerships.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for technology transfer.
- Support the development of national technology transfer policies and strategies.
- Facilitate access to finance for technology transfer projects.

### Green Initiative 8: Gender Equality

**Objective:** To promote gender equality in climate action, including through the integration of a gender perspective into climate policies and programs.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for gender mainstreaming in climate action.
- Support the development of national gender and climate change policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for gender-responsive climate action projects.

### Green Initiative 9: Youth Engagement

**Objective:** To engage youth in climate action, including through the promotion of youth-led initiatives and the integration of a youth perspective into climate policies and programs.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for youth engagement in climate action.
- Support the development of national youth and climate change policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for youth-led climate action projects.

### Green Initiative 10: Indigenous Peoples' Engagement

**Objective:** To engage indigenous peoples in climate action, including through the promotion of indigenous knowledge and the integration of an indigenous perspective into climate policies and programs.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for indigenous engagement in climate action.
- Support the development of national indigenous and climate change policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for indigenous-led climate action projects.

### Green Initiative 11: Local Government Engagement

**Objective:** To engage local governments in climate action, including through the promotion of subnational initiatives and the integration of a local government perspective into climate policies and programs.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for local government engagement in climate action.
- Support the development of national local government and climate change policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for locally-led climate action projects.

### Green Initiative 12: Monitoring, Reporting, and Verification (MRV)

**Objective:** To enhance the capacity of developing countries to monitor, report, and verify their NDCs, including through the development of national MRV systems.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for MRV system development.
- Support the integration of MRV into national climate change policies and programs.
- Facilitate access to finance for MRV projects.

### Green Initiative 13: Adaptation

**Objective:** To enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities and ecosystems to the impacts of climate change, including through improved early warning systems and disaster risk reduction.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for adaptation planning.
- Support the development and implementation of national adaptation plans (NAPs).
- Facilitate access to finance for adaptation projects.

### Green Initiative 14: Mitigation

**Objective:** To reduce greenhouse gas emissions from developing countries, including through the promotion of low emission development strategies (LEDS) and renewable energy.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for mitigation planning.
- Support the development and implementation of national mitigation plans.
- Facilitate access to finance for mitigation projects.

### Green Initiative 15: Loss and Damage

**Objective:** To address loss and damage from climate change impacts, including through enhanced support for vulnerable countries.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for loss and damage planning.
- Support the development of national loss and damage policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for loss and damage projects.

### Green Initiative 16: Transparency

**Objective:** To enhance transparency in climate action, including through the implementation of enhanced transparency frameworks.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for transparency framework implementation.
- Support the development of national transparency policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for transparency projects.

### Green Initiative 17: Finance

**Objective:** To mobilize and manage climate finance, including through the development of national climate finance strategies.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for climate finance strategy development.
- Support the mobilization and management of climate finance.
- Facilitate access to finance for climate action projects.

### Green Initiative 18: Innovation

**Objective:** To promote innovation in climate action, including through the support of research and development (R&D) activities.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for R&D activities.
- Support the development of national innovation policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for innovative climate action projects.

### Green Initiative 19: Education

**Objective:** To enhance education and awareness on climate change, including through the promotion of climate literacy and public engagement.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for climate education initiatives.
- Support the development of national climate education policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for climate education projects.

### Green Initiative 20: Health

**Objective:** To address the health impacts of climate change, including through the promotion of climate-resilient health systems.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for climate-resilient health planning.
- Support the development of national climate and health policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for climate-resilient health projects.

### Green Initiative 21: Agriculture

**Objective:** To enhance the resilience of agricultural systems to the impacts of climate change, including through the promotion of climate-smart agriculture.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for climate-smart agriculture planning.
- Support the development of national climate and agriculture policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for climate-smart agriculture projects.

### Green Initiative 22: Water

**Objective:** To enhance the resilience of water systems to the impacts of climate change, including through the promotion of integrated water resource management.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for integrated water resource management planning.
- Support the development of national climate and water policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for integrated water resource management projects.

### Green Initiative 23: Energy

**Objective:** To promote renewable energy and energy efficiency, including through the development of national energy strategies.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for renewable energy planning.
- Support the development of national climate and energy policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for renewable energy projects.

### Green Initiative 24: Biodiversity

**Objective:** To enhance biodiversity conservation in the context of climate change, including through the promotion of ecosystem-based adaptation.

**Strategies:**
- Provide technical assistance for ecosystem-based adaptation planning.
- Support the development of national climate and biodiversity policies.
- Facilitate access to finance for ecosystem-based adaptation projects.

### Green Initiative 25: Forests

**Objective:** To enhance forest conservation and management in the context of climate change, including through the promotion of REDD+ (Reducing Emissions from Deforestation and Forest Degradation).

**Strategies:**

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.0 PAGE 44

## E

### Project (include specific indicators (project outcomes and outputs))

| Project/Programme Name | Means of Verification (MoV) | Baseline | Mid-term | Final | Target | Assumptions / Note |
|-------------------------|------------------------------|----------|----------|-------|--------|--------------------|
| Green Climate Fund      |                               |          |          |       |        |                   |

### Impact

Support smallholder farmers to increase their agricultural productivity and incomes through climate-resilient farming practices, with additional support for women and youth. This will be achieved by providing training on climate-smart agriculture, access to credit, and improved market opportunities.

### Outputs and outcomes

- Increase the resilience of smallholder farmers to climate change.
- Improve food security and nutrition.
- Enhance livelihoods through increased agricultural productivity.
- Strengthen local institutions for sustainable development.
- Promote gender equality and social inclusion in agriculture.
- Support the development of climate-smart value chains.

### Means of Implementation

- Technical assistance and capacity building for farmers, extension workers, and local governments.
- Access to finance for smallholder farmers through innovative financing mechanisms.
- Development of climate-resilient farming practices and technologies.
- Strengthening of local institutions and policies to support sustainable agriculture.
- Monitoring and evaluation systems to track progress and impact.

### Results Framework

- Increase in agricultural productivity by X%.
- Reduction in post-harvest losses by Y%.
- Increase in the number of farmers adopting climate-smart practices by Z%.
- Improvement in food security indicators by W%.
- Strengthening of local institutions' capacity to support sustainable agriculture by V%.

### Indicators

- Agricultural productivity (tonnes/hectare).
- Post-harvest losses (%).
- Adoption rate of climate-smart practices (%).
- Food security index score.
- Institutional capacity index score.

### Notes

- The project will be implemented in collaboration with local and international partners.
- The project will be aligned with national policies and strategies for agricultural development.
- The project will promote gender equality and social inclusion throughout its implementation.

## Activity 2.2.1
Eco GRF and ecological infrastructure programs to be implemented by the Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR) and local government units (LGUs) are designed to promote the integration of biodiversity conservation, climate change adaptation, and disaster risk reduction and management in development plans, policies, guidelines, and other relevant instruments.

## Activity 2.1.7
Support processes to develop and implement a national biodiversity strategy and action plan (NBSAP).

## Sub-activity 2.2.1.1
Develop and implement a national biodiversity strategy and action plan (NBSAP) that is consistent with the Aichi Biodiversity Targets, the Convention on Biological Diversity (CBD), and other relevant international agreements.

## Sub-activity 2.2.1.2
Support processes to develop and implement a national biodiversity strategy and action plan (NBSAP).

## Sub-activity 2.2.1.3
Conduct national biodiversity assessments, including the development of indicators for monitoring progress towards achieving the Aichi Biodiversity Targets.

## Sub-activity 2.2.1.4
Support processes to develop and implement a national biodiversity strategy and action plan (NBSAP).

## Activity 2.1.7
Support processes to develop and implement a national biodiversity strategy and action plan (NBSAP) that is consistent with the Aichi Biodiversity Targets, the Convention on Biological Diversity (CBD), and other relevant international agreements.

## Sub-activity 2.2.1.5
Conduct national biodiversity assessments, including the development of indicators for monitoring progress towards achieving the Aichi Biodiversity Targets.

## Activity 3.1.4
Promote sustainable management of Eco Funds to ensure that they are used effectively and efficiently in support of climate change adaptation and mitigation activities and accomplish their intended objectives.

Develop guidelines for the effective use of Eco Funds, including criteria for project selection and monitoring and evaluation frameworks.

## Sub-activity 3.1.4.2
Conduct training programs to build capacity among stakeholders in the sustainable management of Eco Funds.

## Sub-activity 3.1.4.3
Monitor and evaluate the use of Eco Funds, including the assessment of their impact on climate change adaptation and mitigation activities.

Promote the development and implementation of Eco Fund policies and guidelines to ensure that they are used effectively and efficiently in support of climate change adaptation and mitigation activities and accomplish their intended objectives.

## Sub-activity 3.1.5.3
Monitor and evaluate the use of Eco Fund policies and guidelines, including the assessment of their impact on climate change adaptation and mitigation activities.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0: PAGE B7

## Section 1.1.1

This section provides an overview of the strategic objectives and management arrangements for the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The GCF is designed to support developing countries in mitigating and adapting to climate change, promoting sustainable development and environmental sustainability.

- **Objective**: To enhance the resilience of developing countries to the adverse effects of climate change.
- **Management Arrangements**:
  - Board oversight
  - Secretariat management
  - Stakeholder engagement

## Section 1.2.1

This section outlines the strategic objectives and management arrangements for the GCF. The GCF is designed to support developing countries in mitigating and adapting to climate change, promoting sustainable development and environmental sustainability.

- **Objective**: To enhance the resilience of developing countries to the adverse effects of climate change.
- **Management Arrangements**:
  - Board oversight
  - Secretariat management
  - Stakeholder engagement

## F: Monitoring, reporting and verification requirements
The monitoring, reporting and verification (MRV) system for this project is based on the principles outlined in the Green Climate Fund's (GCF) MRV requirements. These include:

1. **Baseline**: Establishing a baseline for each indicator.
2. **Monitoring**: Regularly collecting data to track progress against the established baselines.
3. **Reporting**: Compiling and presenting the collected data in a clear and concise manner.
4. **Verification**: Ensuring that the reported data is accurate and reliable.

The reporting requirements for this project are outlined in the Project Document. The verification process will be conducted by an independent third-party auditor approved by the GCF.

Submitted at the end of the 6-month inception phase, the Inception Report will detail the activities undertaken in this phase as well as the establishment of the MRV system. This report will also include a description of any challenges encountered during the development of a workplan for the Operation Phase of the project, which will be approved by the project governance structure.

# RISK ASSESSMENT AND MANAGEMENT

## F-1 Risk factors and mitigation measures (one page):
The risk management process for GCF-funded projects is guided by general risk management principles, which include the identification of risks, assessment of their likelihood and impact, development of appropriate mitigation measures, and monitoring of their effectiveness. The following are some key risk factors that need to be considered in the context of GCF-funded projects:

- **Inadequate or unsustainable project design**: Projects may not achieve their intended objectives if they are not designed properly.
- **Inadequate or unsustainable project implementation**: Projects may fail to deliver expected benefits if implemented poorly.
- **Inadequate or unsustainable project management**: Poor management can lead to delays, cost overruns, and failure to meet objectives.
- **Inadequate or unsustainable project financing**: Insufficient funding can hinder the successful completion of projects.
- **Inadequate or unsustainable project monitoring and evaluation**: Lack of proper monitoring and evaluation can prevent projects from achieving their intended outcomes.

### Mitigation Measures:
These risks can be managed through various measures, including but not limited to:

- **Risk identification**: Regularly identifying potential risks is the first step in managing them.
- **Risk assessment**: Evaluating the likelihood and impact of identified risks helps prioritize mitigation efforts.
- **Risk mitigation planning**: Developing strategies to reduce or eliminate risks.
- **Risk monitoring**: Continuously tracking risks and their impacts.

## F-2 Transparency and Accountability:
The decision-making process for GCF-funded projects must ensure transparency and accountability in the following ways:

- **Public disclosure of information**: All relevant documents, including decisions on funding proposals, should be made publicly available.
- **Independent review**: An independent entity should periodically review the implementation of funded projects to ensure compliance with GCF standards.

### Mitigation Measures:
These measures can include but are not limited to:

- **Regular audits**: Conducting regular financial and performance audits to ensure accountability.
- **Public reporting**: Publishing annual reports on project progress and outcomes.
- **Stakeholder engagement**: Involving stakeholders in the decision-making process to enhance transparency.

## F-3 Integrity and Anti-Corruption:
GCF-funded projects must adhere to high standards of integrity and anti-corruption measures. This includes:

- **Whistleblower protection**: Ensuring that individuals who report corruption or fraud are protected from retaliation.
- **Conflict of interest policies**: Implementing policies to prevent conflicts of interest among decision-makers.

### Mitigation Measures:
These measures can include but are not limited to:

- **Training and awareness-raising**: Providing training on anti-corruption measures and ethical standards.
- **Code of conduct**: Establishing a code of conduct for all stakeholders involved in GCF-funded projects.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1 PAGE 4

## Objective

The objective of this proposal is to provide funding for projects that contribute to climate change mitigation and adaptation in developing countries. The project aims to replicate successful models from the Adapt and Resilience (A&R) program, which has been implemented by SANBI's Adaptation and Mitigation Programmes. This includes projects such as:

- SANBI's Regional Climate Change Programme
- SANBI's National Climate Change Adaptation Strategy
- SANBI's National Climate Change Adaptation Programme
- SANBI's National Climate Change Adaptation Plan

The project aims to be implemented in a manner that is consistent with the Paris Agreement and to contribute to the achievement of national climate change objectives.

# Selected Risk Factor E: Foreign exchange

## Category: Currency

### Probability: Low

#### Description:
The value of the application is in the form of a grant and actual the part will be accrued in US Dollars. The exchange rate at the time of payment will be used to convert the amount into local currency. The project will depend on the foreign exchange arrangements for the grant. This could potentially impact the actual ZAF funds available.

#### Mitigation Measures:
- The exchange rate arrangement will be based on the provider's terms and conditions.
- A budget line will be included in the project budget to cater for any fluctuations in the exchange rate.
- The project will be implemented through the PLAP, which includes foreign and local procurement activities. The Foreign Exchange Committee will monitor the project progress.

### Output

The GCF readiness preparation proposal will be developed and submitted to the GCF.

### Note

- The GCF readiness preparation proposal will be developed by the Ministry of Environment.
- The GCF readiness preparation proposal will be developed in collaboration with UNDP.
- The GCF readiness preparation proposal will be developed in consultation with other relevant stakeholders.
- The GCF readiness preparation proposal will be developed in accordance with the GCF requirements.

### Literature and research

Literature and research on climate change adaptation and mitigation will be reviewed to inform the development of the GCF readiness preparation proposal.

### Stakeholder engagement

Stakeholders will be engaged in the development of the GCF readiness preparation proposal. This will include:

- Consultations with government agencies
- Consultations with civil society organizations
- Consultations with the private sector
- Consultations with other relevant stakeholders

### Lessons learned

Lessons learned from other countries' experiences with the GCF readiness preparation process will be reviewed to inform the development of the proposal.

### Community / Beneficiaries

The project will benefit communities and individuals in the region, particularly those who are vulnerable to the impacts of climate change. The project will also contribute to the achievement of national climate change goals and objectives.

### Budget

The budget for the GCF readiness preparation proposal is $500,000.

### Timeline

The timeline for the development of the GCF readiness preparation proposal is 6 months.

### Success indicators

- Completion of the GCF readiness preparation proposal
- Submission of the GCF readiness preparation proposal to the GCF
- Approval of the GCF readiness preparation proposal by the GCF
- Implementation of the GCF readiness preparation proposal

## Implementation with the UND and large aid communities

The GCF will be used as an engine of the project and will provide an incentive, such for a scheme that requires a financial contribution.

### 327

Women and girls' development is often seen as secondary to the needs and rights of men and boys in the management of natural resources. This has led to the marginalization of women's knowledge and skills in natural resource management, access to land, credit, and technology. Gender inequality is a root cause of poverty. Just as gender equality is essential for sustainable development, so too are the empowerment of women, their full participation in decision-making processes, and the recognition and respect of their rights. The GCF will support efforts to promote gender equality and empower women through activities that address the social, economic, and political dimensions of gender inequality.

### 328

This has extended along the development of the full funding proposal, an assessment of the climate change in women and men. Such analysis is the beginning of the gender mainstreaming process. The GCF will support the integration of gender into the design and implementation of projects. This includes the promotion of gender equality and empowerment of women through activities that address the social, economic, and political dimensions of gender inequality.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.1

## Measuring gender disaggregated outcomes

- Understanding contextual monitoring, evaluation, and learning.
- Gender-responsive climate-smart agriculture (CSA) interventions in the Department of Agriculture (DA) from 2016 to 2021, aligned with the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs).

### SDG 5: Gender equality

- Outcomes regarding gender action plan requirements can be funded.

## Government Stakeholders - MoA

### DA

- DA will be the lead and executing entity for all activities and sub-activities in the project. DA and other implementing agencies will work with the Department of Agriculture (DA), Bureau of Soils and Water Management (BSWM), and the Philippine Coconut Authority (PCA) to ensure that the project is implemented properly.
- The project will be implemented in DA's regional field offices (RFOs). The project will be implemented in provinces where the project is located, with a focus on women-led households. The project will also support the development of gender-responsive climate-smart agriculture (CSA) interventions.

### DENR

- The Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR) will be the lead implementing agency for the project. The DENR will implement the project in collaboration with other government agencies, including the Department of Agriculture (DA), the Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR), and the Department of Social Welfare and Development (DSWD).

### DILG

- The Department of Interior and Local Government (DILG) will be the lead implementing agency for the project. The DILG will implement the project in collaboration with other government agencies, including the Department of Agriculture (DA), the Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR), and the Department of Social Welfare and Development (DSWD).

### NEDA

- The National Economic and Development Authority (NEDA) will be the lead implementing agency for the project. The NEDA will implement the project in collaboration with other government agencies, including the Department of Agriculture (DA), the Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR), and the Department of Social Welfare and Development (DSWD).

## Accounting and Audit

- The accounting and audit requirements are as follows:
  - The project will be audited by the Commission on Audit (COA) in accordance with the rules and regulations of the Government Service Insurance System (GSIS).
  - The project will be audited by the COA in accordance with the rules and regulations of the GSIS.
  - The project will be audited by the COA in accordance with the rules and regulations of the GSIS.

# ANNEXES

## H1 - Mandatory annexes:
